{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\bm{\tilde{\Sigma}^{X}}-\bm{\hat{\Sigma}^{X}_{n}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}\leq\check{\Gamma}(n,p,M,\delta)-\Gamma(n,p,M,\delta)
. Note that

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\bm{\tilde{\Sigma}^{X}}-\bm{\hat{\Sigma}^{X}_{n}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{1\leq k,k^{\prime}\leq p-1}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\frac{1}{n}(\bm{\hat{A}^{X_{k}}})^{\top}\bm{\hat{A}^{X_{k^{\prime}}}}-\frac{1}{n}\bm{(A^{X_{k}}})^{\top}\bm{A^{X_{k^{\prime}}}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{1\leq k,k^{\prime}\leq p-1}\max_{1\leq m,m^{\prime}\leq M}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im}\hat{a}^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}-a^{X_{k}}_{im}a^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}\right|,

(79)

and

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im}\hat{a}^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}-a^{X_{k}}_{im}a^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im}-a^{X_{k}}_{im}\right)\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}-a^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}\right)+\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}a^{X_{k}}_{im}\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}-a^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}a^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im}-a^{X_{k}}_{im}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im}-a^{X_{k}}_{im}\right)\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}-a^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}\right)\right|+\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}a^{X_{k}}_{im}\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}-a^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}a^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im}-a^{X_{k}}_{im}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im}-a^{X_{k}}_{im}\right)^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}-a^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im}\right)^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}-a^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\left(a^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im}-a^{X_{k}}_{im}\right)^{2}}.

(80)

By Lemma
12
with

\displaystyle\Gamma_{g}(j)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\text{max},0}\mathcal{Q}^{2}_{n,p,\delta}\,\text{ for all }j\in[p],

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\phi}(m)

\displaystyle=d_{jm}\sqrt{\frac{(1/c_{1})\log\left(c_{2}/\delta\right)}{n}},

we have

\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im}-a^{X_{k}}_{im}\right)^{2}\leq d^{2}_{j,\text{max}}\sigma_{\text{max},0}\mathcal{Q}^{2}_{n,p,\delta}\frac{(1/c_{1})\log\left(c_{2}/\delta\right)}{n}

for all
m\in[M]
and
k\in[p-1]
. And by
\mathcal{A}_{7}
, we have

\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|a^{X_{k}}_{im}\|^{2}_{2}\leq\Xi_{2}(M)\mathcal{Q}^{3}_{n,p,\delta}

for all
m\in[M]
and
k\in[p-1]
. Combine with (
79
) and (
80
), we have

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\bm{\tilde{\Sigma}^{X}}-\bm{\hat{\Sigma}^{X}_{n}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle d^{2}_{j,\text{max}}\sigma_{\text{max},0}\mathcal{Q}^{2}_{n,p,\delta}\frac{(1/c_{1})\log\left(c_{2}/\delta\right)}{n}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{\Xi_{2}(M)}d_{j,\text{max}}\sqrt{\sigma_{\text{max},0}}\sqrt{\mathcal{Q}^{2}_{n,p,\delta}}\sqrt{\frac{(1/c_{1})\log\left(c_{2}/\delta\right)}{n}}\cdot\sqrt{\mathcal{Q}^{3}_{n,p,M\delta}}

\displaystyle=\check{\Gamma}(n,p,M,\delta)-\Gamma(n,p,M,\delta)\leq\check{\Gamma}(n,p,M,\delta).

Therefore, by (
73
) and Lemma
3
, Lemma
5
, Lemma
9
and Lemma
10
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{2}{n}\max_{k\in[p-1]}\|(\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{u})_{k}\|_{2}\leq\check{\lambda}(n,p,M,\delta)\,\text{and}\,{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\bm{\tilde{\Sigma}^{X}_{n}}-\bm{\Sigma^{X}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}\leq\check{\Gamma}(n,p,M,\delta)\right\}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\mathbb{P}\left\{\cup^{7}_{i=1}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{i}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq 1-8\delta.

Finally, we only need
n
large enough such that
\check{\Gamma}(n,p,M,\delta)\leq\kappa(M)/(32M^{2}s)
and
\check{\chi}(n,p,M,\delta)\leq\nu(M)/3
where
\check{\chi}(n,p,M,\delta)
is defined in (
38
). After dropping
\log(n)
term, to satisfy the first condition, we need

n\geq O\left(\frac{M^{4}s^{2}\log(p^{2}M^{2}/\delta)}{\kappa^{2}(M)}\right),

and to satisfy the second condition, we need

\displaystyle n

\displaystyle\geq O\left(\frac{1}{\kappa(M)\nu^{2}(M)}\max\left\{sM^{2},s\log(p/\delta),sM^{2}\omega^{2}(M)\log(M^{2}p/\delta),\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.s^{3}M^{2}(\log(1/\delta)^{2}),s(d^{2}_{j0}-d^{2}_{js}(M)\Phi^{2}(M))\right\}\right).

Then combine the above results and note that decreasing
8\delta
to
\delta
doesn’t affect the asymptotic order of
n
, we the have the final result.

∎

Appendix B
Useful Lemmas

Recall that
\mathcal{C}_{n,\delta}
is defined in (
34
) and
\mathcal{Q}^{1}_{n,\delta}
,
\mathcal{Q}^{2}_{n,p,\delta}
, and
\mathcal{Q}^{1}_{n,p,M,\delta}
are defined in (
67
).

Lemma 1
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1]
. Then
\max_{k\in[p-1]}\|\bm{A^{X_{k}}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n}\leq\mathcal{C}_{n,\delta}
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof.

The result follows directly from Theorem 6.1 of
Wainwright, 2019
and a union bound.
∎

Lemma 2
.

Suppose
\max_{k\in[p-1]}\|\bm{A^{X_{k}}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n}\leq\mathcal{C}_{n,\delta_{1}}
for some
0<\delta_{1}\leq 1
. Then there exists a constant
c>0
such that

\max_{k\in[p-1]}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}\bm{u}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{M\mathcal{C}_{n,\delta_{1}}\sqrt{\Xi_{1}(M)}}{\sqrt{n}}+2\mathcal{C}_{n,\delta_{1}}\sqrt{\Xi_{1}(M)}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4(p-1)/\delta_{2})}{n}}\\
+\omega(M)\left\{1+7\sqrt{3}\frac{M\sqrt{\log(6(p-1)/\delta_{2})+2\log M}}{\sqrt{n}}+\right.\\
\left.\frac{8c}{3}\frac{M(\log(2n))(\log(6(p-1)/\delta_{2})+2\log M)}{n}\right\}

holds with probability at least
1-\delta_{2}
,
\delta_{2}\in(0,1]
.

Proof.

Note that
\|\bm{Z_{k}}\|_{2}=\|\bm{A^{X_{k}}}\otimes\bm{I_{M}}\|_{2}=\|\bm{A^{X_{k}}}\|_{2}
. Thus
\|\bm{Z_{k}}/\sqrt{n}\|_{2}\leq\mathcal{C}_{n,\delta_{1}}
,
k\in[p-1]
, since
\max_{k\in[p-1]}\|\bm{A^{X_{k}}}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n}\leq\mathcal{C}_{n,\delta_{1}}
. Recall that
\bm{u}=\bm{w}+\bm{r}
, where
\bm{u}
is defined in (
57
),
\bm{w}=(\bm{w_{1}}^{\top},\bm{w_{2}}^{\top},\dots,\bm{w_{n}}^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{nM}
and
\bm{r}=(\bm{r_{1}}^{\top},\bm{r_{2}}^{\top},\dots,\bm{r_{n}}^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{nM}
. Both
\bm{w_{i}}
and
\bm{r_{i}}
are Gaussian vectors with mean zero, and covariance matrices
\bm{\Sigma^{w}}
and
\bm{\Sigma^{r}}
respectively for all
i\in[n]
, where we dropped the superscript
M
to simplify the notation, and
\bm{w_{i}}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}\bm{r_{i}}
.

Fix
k\in[p-1]
. Let
\bm{r_{i}}=\bm{r^{1}_{i}}+\bm{r^{2}_{i}}
, where

\bm{r^{1}_{i}}=\bm{r_{i}}-\bm{\Sigma^{r,X_{k}}}\left(\bm{\Sigma^{X}_{kk}}\right)^{-1}\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bm{r^{2}_{i}}=\bm{\Sigma^{r,X_{k}}}\left(\bm{\Sigma^{X}_{kk}}\right)^{-1}\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}}.

Since both
\bm{r_{i}}
and
\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}}
are Gaussian vectors and
\text{Cov}(\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}},\bm{r^{1}_{i}})=\bm{0}
, we have
\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}\bm{r^{1}_{i}}
. Then

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}\bm{u}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}(\bm{w}+\bm{r^{1}})\right\|_{2}+\left\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}\bm{r^{2}}\right\|_{2}.

(81)

We upper bound the first term on the right hand side of (
81
). Let
\bm{\xi_{i}}=\bm{w_{i}}+\bm{r^{1}_{i}}
. Then
\bm{\xi_{i}}
is a Gaussian vector with mean zero and covariance matrix
\bm{\Sigma^{\xi}_{k}}
, with
\bm{\Sigma^{\xi}_{k}}=\bm{\Sigma^{w}}+\bm{\Sigma^{r}}-\bm{\Sigma^{r,X_{k}}}\left(\bm{\Sigma^{X}_{kk}}\right)^{-1}\bm{\Sigma^{{X_{k}},r}}
. Note that
\bm{\xi}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}\bm{Z_{k}}
and we establish a bound conditional on
\bm{Z_{k}}
first.

For any pair
\bm{\theta},\bm{\theta^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{R}^{nM}
, we have

\left|\left\|\frac{\bm{Z_{k}^{\top}}\bm{\theta}}{n}\right\|_{2}-\left\|\frac{\bm{Z_{k}^{\top}}\bm{\theta}^{\prime}}{n}\right\|_{2}\right|\leq\frac{1}{n}\left\|\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta^{\prime}})\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{n}\|\bm{Z_{k}}\|_{2}\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta^{\prime}}\|_{2}\leq\frac{\mathcal{C}_{n,\delta_{1}}}{\sqrt{n}}\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta^{\prime}}\|_{2}.

Therefore,
\bm{\theta}\mapsto\|\frac{\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}\bm{\theta}}{n}\|_{2}
is a Lipschitz function with a Lipschitz constant
\mathcal{C}_{n,\delta_{1}}/\sqrt{n}
. Note that
\bm{\xi}
has a strongly log-concave distribution with the parameter
(\rho_{max}(\bm{I_{M}}\otimes\bm{\Sigma^{\xi}_{k}}))^{-1}=(\rho_{max}(\bm{\Sigma^{\xi}_{k}}))^{-1}
.
Then, by Theorem 3.16 of
Wainwright, 2019
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\frac{\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}\bm{\xi}}{n}\right\|_{2}\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}\bm{\xi}}{n}\right\|_{2}\right]+\Delta\right\}\leq 2\exp{\left(-\frac{n\Delta^{2}}{4C^{2}_{n,\delta_{1}}\rho_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma^{\xi}_{k}})}\right)}\quad\text{for all }\Delta>0.

(82)

Next, we bound
\mathbb{E}\left[n^{-1}\left\|{\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}\bm{\xi}}\right\|_{2}\right]
using the Sudakov-Fernique inequality
(
Adler and Taylor, 2007
, Theorem 2.2.3 of)
. For any vector
\bm{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{M^{2}}
, let
\zeta_{\bm{\theta}}={n}^{-1}\langle\bm{\theta},\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}\bm{\xi}\rangle
. Then
n^{-1}\|\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}\bm{\xi}\|_{2}=\max_{\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}=1}\zeta_{\bm{\theta}}
. For any two vectors
\bm{\theta},\bm{\theta^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{R}^{M^{2}}
,
we have

\mathbb{E}\left[(\zeta_{\bm{\theta}}-\zeta_{\bm{\theta^{\prime}}})^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{n}\left\langle\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta^{\prime}},\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}\bm{\xi}\right\rangle\right)^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta^{\prime}})^{\top}\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}\bm{\xi}\bm{\xi}^{\top}\bm{Z_{k}}(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta^{\prime}})\right]\\
\leq\frac{\rho_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma^{\xi}_{k}})}{n}\frac{\|\bm{Z_{k}}\|^{2}_{2}}{n}\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta^{\prime}}\|^{2}_{2}\leq\frac{C^{2}_{n,\delta_{1}}\rho_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma^{\xi}_{k}})}{n}\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta^{\prime}}\|^{2}_{2}.

We define another Gaussian process,

\tilde{\zeta}_{\bm{\theta}}=\left(\mathcal{C}_{n,\delta_{1}}\sqrt{\rho_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma^{\xi}_{k}})/n}\right)\cdot\langle\bm{\theta},\bm{\epsilon}\rangle,

where
\bm{\epsilon}\sim N(\bm{0},\bm{I_{M^{2}}})
. For any pair
\bm{\theta},\bm{\theta^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{R}^{M^{2}}
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[(\tilde{\zeta}_{\bm{\theta}}-\tilde{\zeta}_{\bm{\theta^{\prime}}})^{2}\right]=\frac{C^{2}_{n,\delta_{1}}\rho_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma^{\xi}_{k}})}{n}\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta^{\prime}}\|^{2}_{2}\geq\mathbb{E}\left[(\zeta_{\bm{\theta}}-\zeta_{\bm{\theta^{\prime}}})^{2}\right].

Then, by the Sudakov-Fernique inequality, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}\bm{\xi}}{n}\right\|_{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}=1}\zeta_{\bm{\theta}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}=1}\tilde{\zeta}_{\bm{\theta}}\right].

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{\|\bm{\theta}\|_{2}=1}\tilde{\zeta}_{\bm{\theta}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathcal{C}_{n,\delta_{1}}\sqrt{\rho_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma^{\xi}_{k}})}}{\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\epsilon}\|_{2}\right]\leq\frac{\mathcal{C}_{n,\delta_{1}}\sqrt{\rho_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma^{\xi}_{k}})}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{\epsilon}\|^{2}_{2}\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{M\mathcal{C}_{n,\delta_{1}}\sqrt{\rho_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma^{\xi}_{k}})}}{\sqrt{n}}.

Combining (
82
) with the above upper bound, we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\frac{\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}\bm{\xi}}{n}\right\|_{2}\geq\frac{M\mathcal{C}_{n,\delta_{1}}\sqrt{\rho_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma^{\xi}_{k}})}}{\sqrt{n}}+\Delta\right\}\leq 2\exp{\left(-\frac{n\Delta^{2}}{4C^{2}_{n,\delta_{1}}\rho_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma^{\xi}_{k}})}\right)},\quad\Delta>0.

The above inequality holds for any
\bm{Z_{k}}
such that
\|\bm{Z_{k}}/\sqrt{n}\|_{2}\leq\mathcal{C}_{n,\delta_{1}}
and, therefore, is valid unconditionally as well. Since
\Xi_{1}(M)=\max_{k\in[p-1]}\rho_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma^{\xi}_{k}})
, if follows from the union bound that

\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{k\in[p-1]}\;\left\|\frac{\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}\bm{\xi}}{n}\right\|_{2}\geq\frac{M\mathcal{C}_{n,\delta_{1}}\sqrt{\Xi_{1}(M)}}{\sqrt{n}}+\Delta\right\}\leq 2(p-1)\exp{\left(-\frac{n\Delta^{2}}{4C^{2}_{n,\delta_{1}}\Xi_{1}(M)}\right)},\quad\Delta>0.

(83)

Next, we upper bound the second term in (
81
). For any
k\in[p-1]
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}\bm{r^{2}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}(\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}}\otimes\bm{I_{M}})\bm{r^{2}_{i}}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\bm{r^{2}_{i}}(\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}})^{\top}\right\|_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\bm{\Sigma^{r,X_{k}}}\left(\bm{\Sigma^{X}_{kk}}\right)^{-1}\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}}(\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}})^{\top}\right\|_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\bm{\Sigma^{r,X_{k}}}\left(\bm{\Sigma^{X}_{kk}}\right)^{-1}\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}}(\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}})^{\top}-\bm{\Sigma^{r,X_{k}}}\right\|_{\text{F}}+\left\|\bm{\Sigma^{r,X_{k}}}\right\|_{\text{F}}.

\displaystyle=\left\|\bm{\Sigma^{r,X_{k}}}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\left(\bm{\Sigma^{X}_{kk}}\right)^{-1}\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}}(\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}})^{\top}-\bm{I_{M}}\right\}\right\|_{\text{F}}+\left\|\bm{\Sigma^{r,X_{k}}}\right\|_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bm{\Sigma^{r,X_{k}}}\right\|_{\text{F}}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\left(\bm{\Sigma^{X}_{kk}}\right)^{-1}\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}}(\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}})^{\top}-\bm{I_{M}}\right\|_{\text{F}}+\left\|\bm{\Sigma^{r,X_{k}}}\right\|_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\bm{\Sigma^{r,X_{k}}}\right\|_{\text{F}}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\left(\left(\bm{\Sigma^{X}_{kk}}\right)^{-1/2}\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}}\right)\left(\left(\left(\bm{\Sigma^{X}_{kk}}\right)^{-1/2}\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}}\right)\right)^{\top}-\bm{I_{M}}\right\|_{\text{F}}+\left\|\bm{\Sigma^{r,X_{k}}}\right\|_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{\Delta}{=}\left\|\bm{\Sigma^{r,X_{k}}}\right\|_{\text{F}}\left\{\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\bm{b^{X_{k}}_{i}}\left(\bm{b^{X_{k}}_{i}}\right)^{\top}-\bm{I_{M}}\right\|_{\text{F}}+1\right\},

(84)

where
\bm{b^{X_{k}}_{i}}=(\bm{\Sigma^{X}_{kk}})^{-1/2}\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}}\sim N(0,\bm{I_{M}})
. Since

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\bm{b^{X_{k}}_{i}}\left(\bm{b^{X_{k}}_{i}}\right)^{\top}-\bm{I_{M}}\right\|_{\text{F}}\leq M{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\bm{b^{X_{k}}_{i}}\left(\bm{b^{X_{k}}_{i}}\right)^{\top}-\bm{I_{M}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty},

(85)

we only need to bound
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\bm{b^{X_{k}}_{i}}\left(\bm{b^{X_{k}}_{i}}\right)^{\top}-\bm{I_{M}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}
.
We use Theorem 4.1 in
Kuchibhotla and Chakrabortty, 2022
to bound this term. We first check the conditions therein. Note that for
b^{X_{k}}_{im}
is a standard normal random variable for all
i\in[n]
and
m\in[M]
, we have
(b^{X_{k}}_{im})^{2}
to be chi-squared distributed with degree of freedom 1. By the moment generating function of chi-square distribution, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\{\eta(b^{X_{k}}_{im})^{2}\}\right]=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-2\eta}}\quad\text{for all }0<\eta<1/2.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\|b^{X_{k}}_{im}\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle=\inf\{\eta>0:\mathbb{E}\left[{\psi_{2}(|b^{X_{k}}_{im}|/\eta)}\right]\leq 1\}

\displaystyle=\inf\{\eta>0:\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp((b^{X_{k}}_{im})^{2}/\eta^{2})}\right]\leq 2\}

\displaystyle=\inf\{\eta>0:1/\sqrt{1-2/\eta^{2}}\leq 2\}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{3}}.

Furthermore, we have

\|b^{X_{k}}_{i}\|_{M,\psi_{2}}=\max_{m\in[M]}\|b^{X_{k}}_{im}\|_{\psi_{2}}=\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{3}}.

for all
i\in[n]
. In addition, note that

\Var{\left(b^{X_{k}}_{im}b^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}}\right)}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[(b^{X_{k}}_{im})^{2}(b^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}})^{2}\right]\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\left[(b^{X_{k}}_{im})^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[(b^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}})^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}=3

for all
m,m^{\prime}\in[M]
, thus we have

\max_{m,m^{\prime}\in[M]}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\Var{\left(b^{X_{k}}_{im}b^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}}\right)}\right\}\leq 3.

Therefore, by Theorem 4.1 in
Kuchibhotla and Chakrabortty, 2022
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\bm{b^{X_{k}}_{i}}\left(\bm{b^{X_{k}}_{i}}\right)^{\top}-\bm{I_{M}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}>7\sqrt{3}\sqrt{\frac{\Delta+2\log M}{n}}+\frac{8c}{3}\frac{(\log(2n))(\Delta+2\log M)}{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 3e^{-\Delta},

for all
\Delta>0
, where
c
is a constant.
Thus, combining the above equation with (
84
) and (
85
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\left\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}\bm{r^{2}}\right\|_{2}>\left\|\bm{\Sigma^{r,X_{k}}}\right\|_{\text{F}}\left\{1+7\sqrt{3}\frac{M\sqrt{\Delta+2\log M}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{8c}{3}\frac{M(\log(2n))(\Delta+2\log M)}{n}\right\}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 3e^{-\Delta}.

Let
\omega(M)=\max_{k\in[p-1]}\left\|\bm{\Sigma^{r,X_{k}}}\right\|_{\text{F}}
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{k\in[p-1]}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{Z_{k}}^{\top}\bm{r^{2}}\right\|_{2}>\omega(M)\left(1+7\sqrt{3}\frac{M\sqrt{\Delta+2\log M}}{\sqrt{n}}\right.\right.

(86)

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\frac{8c}{3}\frac{M(\log(2n))(\Delta+2\log M)}{n}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 3(p-1)e^{-\Delta}.

The result follows by combining (
81
), (
83
), and (
86
).
∎

Lemma 3
.

Let
\lambda_{n}=\tilde{\lambda}(n,p,M,\delta)
, where

\tilde{\lambda}(n,p,M,\delta)=2\mathcal{C}_{n,\delta}\left(\frac{M\sqrt{\Xi_{1}(M)}}{\sqrt{n}}+2\sqrt{\Xi_{1}(M)}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4(p-1)/\delta)}{n}}\right)\\
+2\omega(M)\left\{7\sqrt{3}\frac{M\sqrt{\log(6(p-1)/\delta)+2\log M}}{\sqrt{n}}\right.\\
\left.+\frac{8Mc(\log(2n))(\log(6(p-1)/\delta)+2\log M)}{3n}\right\},

is defined in (
54
), then (
62
) holds with probability at least
1-2\delta
. That is, we have

\lambda_{n}\geq\frac{2}{n}\max_{k\in[p-1]}\|(\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{u})_{k}\|_{2}

hold with probability at least
1-2\delta
.

Proof.

The result follows directly from Lemma
1
, Lemma
2
and Bonferroni inequality.
∎

Lemma 4
.

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sqrt{\sum^{p-1}_{k=1}\left\|\bm{\hat{B}_{k}}-\bm{B^{*}_{k}}\right\|^{2}_{\text{F}}}\leq\chi(n,p,M,\delta)+12\sqrt{s}\frac{\omega(M)}{\sqrt{\kappa(M)}}\right\}\geq 1-3\delta,

where
\kappa(M)
is defined in (
28
) and
\chi(n,p,M,\delta)
is defined in (
32
).

Proof.

By Lemma
3
and (
64
), we can get an error bound for
\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{\ast}\|_{2}
by applying Theorem 1 in
Negahban et al., 2012
.
Note that by Lemma C.4 of
Zhao et al., 2019
, we have the subspace compatibility constant defined in Definition 3 of
Negahban et al., 2012
to be
\sqrt{s}
.
Then, for all
0<\delta\leq 1
, when
n
is large enough such that
\Gamma(n,p,M,\delta)\leq\kappa(M)/(32M^{2}s)
, where
\Gamma(n,p,M,\delta)
is defined in (
88
), and let
\lambda_{n}=\tilde{\lambda}(n,p,M,\delta)
, where
\tilde{\lambda}(n,p,M,\delta)
is defined in (
33
), then by Corollary 1 in
Negahban et al., 2012
, Lemma
3
, (
64
), and a union bound, we have the desired result.
∎

Lemma 5
.

There exists a constant
c
such that

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\bm{\hat{\Sigma}^{X}_{n}}-\bm{\Sigma^{X}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}\leq 7\sqrt{3}K_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log{(3/\delta)}+2\log{((p-1)M)}}{n}}\\
+\frac{8cK_{0}\log{(2n)}(\log{(3/\delta)}+2\log{((p-1)M)})}{3n}

(87)

holds with probability at least
1-\delta
where
K_{0}
is defined by (
27
).

Proof.

The result follows Theorem 4.1 in
Kuchibhotla and Chakrabortty, 2022
. In order to apply the theorem, we need to check the conditions therein. First, we bound
\max_{k\in[p-1],m\in[M]}\|a^{X_{k}}_{im}\|_{\psi_{2}}
. Note that for
\zeta\sim N(0,\sigma^{2})
, we have
(\zeta/\sigma)^{2}
is chi-square distributed with degree of freedom 1. By the moment generating function of chi-square distribution, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\{\eta(\zeta/\sigma)^{2}\}\right]=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-2\eta}}\quad\text{for all }0<\eta<1/2.

Thus, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\zeta^{2}/t^{2}\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left\{\left(\sigma^{2}/t^{2}\right)\cdot\left(\zeta^{2}/\sigma^{2}\right)\right\}\right]=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-2\sigma^{2}/t^{2}}}

for all
t>\sqrt{2}\sigma
. Let
1/\sqrt{1-2\sigma^{2}/t^{2}}\leq 2
, we have
t\geq(2\sqrt{2})/(\sqrt{3}\sigma)
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\|\zeta\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle=\inf\{t>0:\mathbb{E}\left[{\psi_{2}(|\zeta|/t)}\right]\leq 1\}

\displaystyle=\inf\{t>0:\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp(\zeta^{2}/t^{2})}\right]\leq 2\}

\displaystyle=\inf\{t>0:1/\sqrt{1-2\sigma^{2}/t^{2}}\leq 2\}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{3}}\sigma.

Based on the above result, we have for any
i\in[n]
,

\displaystyle\|\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}}\|_{M,\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle=\max_{k\in[p-1],m\in[M]}\|a^{X_{k}}_{im}\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{3}}\max_{k\in[p-1],m\in[M]}\sqrt{\Sigma^{X}_{kk,mm}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{3}}\sqrt{K_{0}},

where
K_{0}
is defined by (
27
).
We then bound
\max_{k,k^{\prime}\in[p],m,m^{\prime}\in[M]}\Var{\left(a^{X_{k}}_{im}a^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}\right)}
. This followed by

\displaystyle\Var{\left(a^{X_{k}}_{im}a^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[(a^{X_{k}}_{im})^{2}(a^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}})^{2}\right]\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\left[(a^{X_{k}}_{im})^{4}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[(a^{X_{k^{\prime}}}_{im^{\prime}})^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\left(9(\Sigma^{X}_{kk,mm})^{2}(\Sigma^{X}_{k^{\prime}k^{\prime},m^{\prime}m^{\prime}})^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq 3K_{0}^{2}.

Thus, the final result is derived by applying Theorem 4.1 in
Kuchibhotla and Chakrabortty, 2022
with
K_{n,p}=(2\sqrt{2}/\sqrt{3})\sqrt{K_{0}}
and
A^{2}_{n,p}=3K_{0}^{2}
.
∎

Lemma 6
.

For
\bm{x_{i}},\bm{y_{i}}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
,
i\in[n]
, we have

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\bm{x_{i}}\bm{y_{i}}^{\top}\right\|_{\text{F}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|\bm{x_{i}}\|^{2}_{2}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|\bm{y_{i}}\|^{2}_{2}}\,.

Proof.

For any
m,m^{\prime}\in[M]
, we have

\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}x_{im}y_{im^{\prime}}\right|^{2}\leq\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}x^{2}_{im}\right)\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}y^{2}_{im^{\prime}}\right).

Therefore,

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\bm{x_{i}}\bm{y_{i}}^{\top}\right\|^{2}_{\text{F}}=\sum^{M}_{m,m^{\prime}=1}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}x_{im}y_{im^{\prime}}\right|^{2}\leq\sum^{M}_{m,m^{\prime}=1}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}x^{2}_{im}\right)\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}y^{2}_{im^{\prime}}\right)\\
=\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\sum^{M}_{m=1}x^{2}_{im}\right)\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\sum^{M}_{m^{\prime}=1}y^{2}_{im^{\prime}}\right)=\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|\bm{x_{i}}\|^{2}_{2}\right)\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|\bm{y_{i}}\|^{2}_{2}\right),

and the result immediately follows.
∎

Lemma 7
.

Let

\Gamma(n,p,M,\delta)=7\sqrt{3}K_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log{(3/\delta)}+2\log{((p-1)M)}}{n}}\\
+\frac{8cK_{0}\log{(2n)}(\log{(3/\delta)}+2\log{((p-1)M)})}{3n},

(88)

then when
\Gamma(n,p,M,\delta)\leq\kappa(M)/(32M^{2}s)
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{2n}\|\bm{Z}\bm{\Delta\beta}\|^{2}_{2}\geq\frac{\kappa}{4}\|\bm{\Delta\beta}\|^{2}_{2}\text{ for all }\bm{\Delta\beta}\in\mathbb{C}(\mathscr{N}_{j})\right\}\geq 1-\delta,

where
\mathbb{C}(\mathscr{N}_{j})
is defined in (
63
).

Proof.

We want to first prove

\frac{1}{2n}\|\bm{Z}\bm{\Delta\beta}\|^{2}_{2}\geq\kappa_{\mathcal{L}}\|\bm{\Delta\beta}\|^{2}_{2}-\tau_{\mathcal{L}}(\bm{\beta^{\ast}})\quad\text{for all }\bm{\Delta\beta}\in\mathbb{C}(\mathscr{N}_{j}),

where
\kappa_{\mathcal{L}}>0
is a positive constant,
\tau_{\mathcal{L}}(\beta^{\ast})>0
, and
\mathbb{C}(\mathscr{N}_{j})
is defined in (
63
).

Since
\bm{Z}=\bm{A^{X}}\otimes\bm{I_{M}}
, thus for any
\bm{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p-1)M^{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\|\bm{Z}\bm{\theta}\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\|(\bm{A^{X}}\otimes I_{M})\bm{\theta}\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\bm{\theta}^{\top}(\bm{A^{X}}\otimes\bm{I_{M}})^{\top}(\bm{A^{X}}\otimes\bm{I_{M}})\bm{\theta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\bm{\theta}^{\top}((\bm{A^{X}})^{\top}\otimes\bm{I_{M}})(\bm{A^{X}}\otimes\bm{I_{M}})\bm{\theta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\bm{\theta}^{\top}((\bm{A^{X}})^{\top}\bm{A^{X}}\otimes\bm{I_{M}})\bm{\theta}

\displaystyle=\bm{\theta}^{\top}(\bm{\hat{\Sigma}^{X}_{n}}\otimes\bm{I_{M}})\bm{\theta},

where
\bm{\hat{\Sigma}^{X}_{n}}=\frac{1}{n}(\bm{A^{X}})^{\top}\bm{A^{X}}
. We then further have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\|\bm{Z}\bm{\theta}\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=\bm{\theta}^{\top}(\bm{\Sigma^{X}}\otimes\bm{I_{M}})\bm{\theta}+\bm{\theta}^{\top}\left((\bm{\hat{\Sigma}^{X}_{n}}-\bm{\Sigma^{X}})\otimes\bm{I_{M}}\right)\bm{\theta}

\displaystyle\geq\left|\bm{\theta}^{\top}(\bm{\Sigma^{X}}\otimes\bm{I_{M}})\bm{\theta}\right|-\left|\bm{\theta}^{\top}\left((\bm{\hat{\Sigma}^{X}_{n}}-\bm{\Sigma^{X}})\otimes\bm{I_{M}}\right)\bm{\theta}\right|.

Note that

\displaystyle\min_{\bm{\theta}\in\mathbb{C}(\mathscr{N}_{j})\setminus\{0\}}\frac{\bm{\theta}^{\top}(\bm{\Sigma^{X}}\otimes\bm{I_{M}})\bm{\theta}}{\|\bm{\theta}\|^{2}_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\min_{\bm{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\{0\}}\frac{\bm{\theta}^{\top}(\bm{\Sigma^{X}}\otimes\bm{I_{M}})\bm{\theta}}{\|\bm{\theta}\|^{2}_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\rho_{\min}\left(\bm{\Sigma^{X}_{\mathscr{N}_{j}\mathscr{N}_{j}}}\otimes\bm{I_{M}}\right)

\displaystyle=\rho_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma^{X}_{\mathscr{N}_{j}\mathscr{N}_{j}}})

\displaystyle=\kappa,

and we have
\kappa(M)>0
for all
M
by Assumption
3
.
Thus, for any
\bm{\theta}\in\mathbb{C}(\mathscr{N}_{j})
, we have

\frac{1}{n}\|\bm{Z}\bm{\theta}\|^{2}_{2}\geq\kappa\|\bm{\theta}\|^{2}_{2}-\left|\bm{\theta}^{\top}\left((\bm{\hat{\Sigma}^{X}_{n}}-\bm{\Sigma^{X}})\otimes\bm{I_{M}}\right)\bm{\theta}\right|.

(89)

To prove the RSC condition, it then suffices to give an upper bound for

|\bm{\theta}^{\top}\left((\bm{\hat{\Sigma}^{X}_{n}}-\bm{\Sigma^{X}})\otimes\bm{I_{M}}\right)\bm{\theta}|,

where
\bm{\theta}\in\mathbb{C}(\mathscr{N}_{j})
. By Lemma 5 in section D of the appendix of
Zhao et al., 2019
and the definition of
\mathbb{C}(\mathscr{N}_{j})
, for any
\bm{\theta}\in\mathbb{C}(\mathscr{N}_{j})
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\bm{\theta}^{\top}\left((\bm{\hat{\Sigma}^{X}_{n}}-\bm{\Sigma^{X}})\otimes\bm{I_{M}}\right)\bm{\theta}\right|

\displaystyle\leq M^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|(\bm{\hat{\Sigma}^{X}_{n}}-\bm{\Sigma^{X}})\otimes\bm{I_{M}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}\|\bm{\theta}\|^{2}_{1,2}

\displaystyle=M^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|(\bm{\hat{\Sigma}^{X}_{n}}-\bm{\Sigma^{X}})\otimes\bm{I_{M}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}\left(\|\bm{\theta}_{\mathcal{M}}\|_{1,2}+\|\bm{\theta}_{\mathcal{M}^{\bot}}\|_{1,2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 16M^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|(\bm{\hat{\Sigma}^{X}_{n}}-\bm{\Sigma^{X}})\otimes I_{M}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}\|\bm{\theta}_{\mathcal{M}}\|^{2}_{1,2}

\displaystyle\leq 16sM^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|(\bm{\hat{\Sigma}^{X}_{n}}-\bm{\Sigma^{X}})\otimes I_{M}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}\|\bm{\theta}_{\mathcal{M}}\|^{2}_{1,2},

where the penultimate line is by Lemma 6 in section D of
Zhao et al., 2019
. Note that

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|(\bm{\hat{\Sigma}^{X}_{n}}-\bm{\Sigma^{X}})\otimes\bm{I_{M}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\bm{\hat{\Sigma}^{X}_{n}}-\bm{\Sigma^{X}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty},

then combine (
89
) and Lemma
5
, we have constant
c
such that for any
\delta>0
, with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n}\|\bm{Z}\bm{\Delta\beta}\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{\kappa}{2}-8M^{2}s\cdot\left\{7\sqrt{3}K_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log{(3/\delta)}+2\log{((p-1)M)}}{n}}+\right.\right.

(90)

\displaystyle\left.\left.\frac{8cK_{0}\log{(2n)}(\log{(3/\delta)}+2\log{((p-1)M)})}{3n}\right\}\right)\|\bm{\Delta\beta}\|^{2}_{2}

for all
\bm{\Delta\beta}\in\mathbb{C}(\mathscr{N}_{j})
.
Let
\Gamma(n,p,M,\delta)
be defined by (
88
),
then when
\Gamma(n,p,M,\delta)\leq\kappa(M)/(32M^{2}s)
, we have

\frac{1}{2n}\|\bm{Z}\bm{\Delta\beta}\|^{2}_{2}\geq\frac{\kappa}{4}\|\bm{\Delta\beta}\|^{2}_{2}\text{ for all }\bm{\Delta\beta}\in\mathbb{C}(\mathscr{N}_{j})

with probability at least
1-\delta
.

∎

In the next few results, recall that

\mathcal{Q}^{1}_{n,\delta}=1+8\left(\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{n}}\right).

Lemma 8
.

Let
\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{n}
be i.i.d. mean zero random elements in some Hilbert space, and
\mathbb{E}[\|\xi_{1}\|^{2}]=\sigma_{\xi}
. Besides, we assume that

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\xi_{1}\|^{2k}\right]\leq(2\sigma_{\xi})^{k}\cdot k!\quad\text{ for all }k=1,2,\cdots.

Then for any given
\delta\in(0,1]
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|\xi_{i}\|^{2}\leq\sigma_{\xi}\mathcal{Q}^{1}_{n,\delta}\right\}\geq 1-\delta.

Proof.

Note that
x\mapsto|x|^{k}
is a convex function when
k\geq 1
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
. By Jensen’s inequality, we have

|x/2+y/2|^{k}\leq(|x|^{k}+|y|^{k})/2
, which implies that
|x+y|^{k}\leq 2^{k-1}(|x|^{k}+|y|^{k})
.
Then by Lemma
11
, for
k\geq 2
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\|\xi_{1}\|^{2}-\sigma_{\xi}\right|^{k}\right]\leq 2^{k-1}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|\xi_{1}\|^{2k}\right]+\sigma^{k}_{\xi}\right)\leq 2^{k-1}\left((2\sigma_{\xi})^{k}k!+\sigma^{k}_{\xi}\right)\leq(4\sigma_{\xi})^{k}k!.

Thus, we have

\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\|\xi_{i}\|^{2}-\sigma_{\xi}\right|^{k}\right]\leq\frac{k!}{2}(32\sigma^{2}_{\xi})(4\sigma_{\xi})^{k-2}

for all
k=2,3,\dots
. Then, by Theorem 2.5
Bosq, 2000
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|\xi_{i}\|^{2}-\sigma_{\xi}\right|>\Delta\right\}\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{n\Delta^{2}}{64\sigma^{2}_{\xi}+8\sigma_{\xi}\Delta}\right),\quad\Delta>0.

The result follows by rearranging the terms.
∎

Lemma 9
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1]
. For any
j\in[p]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|g_{ij}\|^{2}\leq\sigma_{\max,0}\mathcal{Q}^{1}_{n,\delta}\right\}\geq 1-\delta

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|e_{ij}\|^{2}\leq\sigma_{jr}\mathcal{Q}^{1}_{n,\delta}\right\}\geq 1-\delta,

where
e_{ij}
is defined in (
5
) and
\sigma_{jr}=\mathbb{E}[\|e_{ij}\|^{2}]
.

Proof.

The result follows directly from Lemma
8
.
∎

Lemma 10
.

For all
0<\delta\leq 1
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|\bm{r_{i}}+\bm{w_{i}}\|^{2}_{2}\leq\Xi_{4}(M)\mathcal{Q}^{1}_{n,\delta}\right\}\geq 1-\delta,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|\bm{a^{X_{k}}}_{i}\|^{2}_{2}\leq\tr\left(\bm{\Sigma^{X}_{kk}}\right)\mathcal{Q}^{1}_{n,\delta}\right\}\geq 1-\delta,

and

\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|a^{X_{k}}_{im}\|^{2}_{2}\leq\Xi_{2}(M)\mathcal{Q}^{1}_{n,\delta}\right\}\geq 1-\delta\,\text{for all }m\in[M],k\in[p-1].

Proof.

Note that
\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{r_{i}}+\bm{w_{i}}\|^{2}_{2}]=\Xi_{4}(M)
,
\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}}\|^{2}_{2}]=\tr(\bm{\Sigma^{X}_{kk}})
and
\mathbb{E}[\|a^{X_{k}}_{im}\|^{2}_{2}]=\Sigma^{X_{k}X_{k}}_{mm}\leq\rho_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma^{X}_{kk}})\leq\Xi_{2}(M)
, where
\Xi_{2}(M),\Xi_{4}(M)
is defined in (
26
), and then the result follows directly from Lemma
8
.
∎

Lemma 11
.

Let
g
be a mean zero random Gaussian function in the Hilbert space
\mathbb{H}
. Let
\sigma_{0}=\mathbb{E}\left[\|g\|^{2}\right]
.
Then

\mathbb{E}\left[\|g\|^{2k}\right]\leq(2\sigma_{0})^{k}\cdot k!\quad\text{ for all }k=1,2,\cdots.

Proof.

Let
\{\phi_{m}\}_{m\geq 1}
be othornormal eigenfunctions of covariance function of
g
.
Let
a_{m}=\langle g,\phi_{m}\rangle
. We have
a_{m}
,
m\geq 1
, are independent mean zero Gaussian random variables with variance
\sigma_{m}
and
\sigma_{0}=\sum_{m\geq 1}\sigma_{m}
. We further have

g=\sum^{\infty}_{m=1}\sigma_{m}^{1/2}\xi_{m}\phi_{m},

where
\xi_{m}=\sigma^{-1/2}_{m}a_{m}
are independent standard Gaussian, and

\|g\|=\left(\sum_{m\geq 1}\sigma_{m}\xi^{2}_{m}\right)^{1/2}
.

Using the Jensen’s inequality for
t\mapsto t^{2k}
, we have

\|g\|^{2k}=\left(\sum_{m\geq 1}\sigma_{m}\right)^{k}\cdot\left(\frac{\sum_{m\geq 1}\sigma_{m}\xi^{2}_{m}}{\sum_{m\geq 1}\sigma_{m}}\right)^{k}\\
\leq\left(\sum_{m\geq 1}\sigma_{m}\right)^{k}\cdot\frac{\sum_{m\geq 1}\sigma_{m}\xi^{2k}_{m}}{\sum_{m\geq 1}\sigma_{m}}=\left(\sum_{m\geq 1}\sigma_{m}\right)^{k-1}\cdot\left(\sum_{m\geq 1}\sigma_{m}\xi^{2k}_{m}\right).

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|g\|^{2k}\right]\leq\left(\sum_{m\geq 1}\sigma_{m}\right)^{k-1}\cdot\left(\sum_{m\geq 1}\sigma_{m}\mathbb{E}\left[\xi^{2k}_{m}\right]\right)=\sigma_{0}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\xi^{2k}_{1}\right]\leq\sigma_{0}^{k}\cdot 2^{k}\cdot k!=(2\sigma_{0})^{k}k!\,,

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 12
.

Recall that
\bm{g_{i}}(\cdot)=(g_{i1}(\cdot),g_{i2}(\cdot),\dots,g_{ip}(\cdot))^{\top}
is our
i
-th observation defined in Section
2.1
. Besides, recall that in Section
2.4
, we have
\phi_{m}=\phi_{jm}
and
\hat{\phi}_{m}=\hat{\phi}_{jm}
be the
m
-th basis function and and its corresponding estimate respectively used to do projection for
j
-th node, and

\bm{\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{i,M}}=(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{i1},\ldots,\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{iM})^{\top}
be the projection score vector of
\bm{g_{i}}
by using
\{\hat{\phi}_{m}\}^{M}_{m=1}
.
Under the assumption that

\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|g_{ij}\|^{2}\leq\Gamma_{g}(j)\,\text{ for all }j\in[p],

and

\|\hat{\phi}_{m}-\phi_{m}\|\leq\Gamma_{\phi}(m)\,\text{ for all }m\geq 1,

for some
0<\delta\leq 1
, we then have

\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im}-a^{X_{k}}_{im}\right)^{2}\leq\Gamma_{g}(k)\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m),

for all
k\in[p-1]
and
m\geq 1
. Furthermore, we have

\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|\bm{\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{i}}-\bm{a^{X_{k}}_{i}}\|^{2}\leq\Gamma_{g}(k)\sum^{M}_{m=1}\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m).

Proof.

Note that

\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im}-a^{X_{k}}_{im}\right)^{2}=\left(\langle g^{X_{k}}_{i},\hat{\phi}_{m}-\phi_{m}\rangle\right)^{2}\leq\|g^{X_{k}}_{i}\|^{2}\|\hat{\phi}_{m}-\phi_{m}\|^{2}.

Thus we have

\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im}-a^{X_{k}}_{im}\right)^{2}\leq\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|g^{X_{k}}_{i}\|^{2}\right)\|\hat{\phi}_{m}-\phi_{m}\|^{2}.

The rest of the proof follows directly from the assumptions we made.
∎

Lemma 13
.

For a given node
j\in[p]
, recall that
\phi_{m}=\phi_{jm}
and
\hat{\phi}_{m}=\hat{\phi}_{jm}
are the
m
-th basis function and its corresponding estimate respectively used to do projection for
j
-th node, defined in Section
2.4
. Besides, let
\beta_{k}(t^{\prime},t)=\beta_{jk}(t^{\prime},t)
and recall that
b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}}=\int_{\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{T}}\beta_{k}(t^{\prime},t)\phi_{m}(t)\phi_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}dt
is defined in (
7
) and
\tilde{b}_{k,mm^{\prime}}=\int_{\mathcal{T}}\beta_{k}(t^{\prime},t)\hat{\phi}_{m}(t^{\prime})\hat{\phi}_{m^{\prime}}(t)dt^{\prime}dt
is defined in (
44
).
Under the assumption that

\|\hat{\phi}_{m}-\phi_{m}\|\leq\Gamma_{\phi}(m)\,\text{for all }m\geq 1,

and
\sum^{\infty}_{m=1}\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m)<\infty
, then we have

\sum^{p-1}_{k=1}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=1}\left(\tilde{b}_{k,mm^{\prime}}-b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}}\right)^{2}\leq 2\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\|\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\|^{2}_{\text{HS}}\right)\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m)\\
+2\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=1}\left|\int_{\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{T}}\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\phi_{m}(t)\left(\hat{\phi}_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})-\phi_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})\right)dt^{\prime}dt\right|^{2},

where
\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})
is defined in (
5
).

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle\quad\left|\tilde{b}_{k,mm^{\prime}}-b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{T}}\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\left(\hat{\phi}_{m}(t)\hat{\phi}_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})-\phi_{m}(t)\phi_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})\right)dt^{\prime}dt\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{T}}\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\left\{\left(\hat{\phi}_{m}(t)-\phi_{m}(t)\right)\hat{\phi}_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})+\phi_{m}(t)\left(\hat{\phi}_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})-\phi_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})\right)\right\}dt^{\prime}dt\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int_{\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{T}}\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\left(\hat{\phi}_{m}(t)-\phi_{m}(t)\right)\hat{\phi}_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}dt\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\int_{\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{T}}\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\phi_{m}(t)\left(\hat{\phi}_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})-\phi_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})\right)dt^{\prime}dt\right|.

(91)

Since
\{\phi_{m^{\prime}}\}^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=1}
is an orthonormal function basis, thus when we treat

\int_{\mathcal{T}}\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\left(\hat{\phi}_{m}(t)-\phi_{m}(t)\right)dt

as a function of
t^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=1}\left|\int_{\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{T}}\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\left(\hat{\phi}_{m}(t)-\phi_{m}(t)\right)\hat{\phi}_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}dt\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{T}}\left(\int_{\mathcal{T}}\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\left(\hat{\phi}_{m}(t)-\phi_{m}(t)\right)dt\right)^{2}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{T}}\left(\int_{\mathcal{T}}\beta^{2}_{k}(t,t^{\prime})dt\right)\left(\int_{\mathcal{T}}\left(\hat{\phi}_{m}(t)-\phi_{m}(t)\right)^{2}dt\right)dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\|\hat{\phi}_{m}-\phi_{m}\|^{2}\|\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\|^{2}_{\text{HS}}

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m)\|\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\|^{2}_{\text{HS}}.

(92)

Combine (
91
)-(
92
), and note that
b^{*}_{k,mm^{\prime}}=\tilde{b}_{k,mm^{\prime}}=0
for all
m,m^{\prime}\geq 1
when
k\notin\mathscr{N}_{j}
,
thus we have

\sum^{p-1}_{k=1}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=1}\left(\tilde{b}_{k,mm^{\prime}}-b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}}\right)^{2}\leq 2\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\|\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\|^{2}_{\text{HS}}\right)\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m)\\
+2\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=1}\left|\int_{\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{T}}\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\phi_{m}(t)\left(\hat{\phi}_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})-\phi_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})\right)dt^{\prime}dt\right|^{2}.

∎

Lemma 14
.

Recall that
\bm{g_{i}}(\cdot)=(g_{i1}(\cdot),g_{i2}(\cdot),\dots,g_{ip}(\cdot))^{\top}
is our
i
-th observation defined in Section
2.1
and
e_{ij}(\cdot)
is the error term defined in (
5
). Besides, recall that
\phi_{m}=\phi_{jm}
and
\hat{\phi}_{m}=\hat{\phi}_{jm}
are the
m
-th basis function and its corresponding estimate respectively used to do projection for the
j
-th node, defined in Section
2.4
. Recall from (
45
)

\bm{v_{iM}}=\sum^{p-1}_{k=1}(\bm{\tilde{B}_{k,M}}-\bm{B^{\ast}_{k,M}})\bm{\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{i,M}}+(\bm{\tilde{r}_{i,M}}-\bm{r_{i,M}})+(\bm{\tilde{w}_{i,M}}-\bm{w_{i,M}}),

and suppose that

\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|g_{ij}\|^{2}\leq\Gamma_{g}(j)\,\text{ for all }j\in[p],

\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|e_{ij}\|^{2}\leq\Gamma_{e}\text{ for all }j\in[p],

and

\|\hat{\phi}_{m}-\phi_{m}\|\leq\Gamma_{\phi}(m)\,\text{ for all }m\geq 1,

where
\sum^{\infty}_{m=1}\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m)<\infty
. Then,

\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}v^{2}_{im}\leq\text{I}_{m}+\text{II}_{m}+\text{III}_{m},

(93)

where

\displaystyle\text{I}_{m}

\displaystyle=6\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\Gamma_{g}(k)\right)\left\{\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\|\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\|^{2}_{\text{HS}}\right)\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.+\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=1}\left|\int_{\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{T}}\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\phi_{m}(t)\left(\hat{\phi}_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})-\phi_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})\right)dt^{\prime}dt\right|^{2}\right\}.

\displaystyle\text{II}_{m}

\displaystyle=3\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=M+1}\left(b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}}\right)^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\Gamma_{g}(k)\right)\left(\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=M+1}\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m^{\prime})\right),

\displaystyle\text{III}_{m}

\displaystyle=3\Gamma_{e}\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m).

Recall that
\beta_{jk}(t,t^{\prime})
is defined in (
5
). Drop the subscript
j
and let
\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})=\beta_{jk}(t,t^{\prime})
. Recall that
b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}}=\int_{\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{T}}\beta_{k}(t^{\prime},t)\phi_{m}(t)\phi_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}dt
is defined in (
7
).
Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|\bm{v_{i}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 12\left(\sum^{\infty}_{m=1}\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m)\right)\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\|\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\|^{2}_{\text{HS}}\right)\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\Gamma_{g}(k)\right)

(94)

\displaystyle+3\Phi^{2}(M)\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\Gamma_{g}(k)\right)\left(\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=M+1}\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m^{\prime})\right)+3\Gamma_{e}\sum^{M}_{m=1}\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m),

where, as previously defined in (
37
),

\Phi(M)=\sqrt{\sum^{p-1}_{k=1}\sum^{M}_{m=1}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=M+1}\left(b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}.

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle v_{im}

\displaystyle=\sum^{p-1}_{k=1}\sum^{M}_{m^{\prime}=1}(\tilde{b}_{k,mm^{\prime}}-b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}})\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}}+\sum^{p-1}_{k=1}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=M+1}\left(\tilde{b}_{k,mm^{\prime}}\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}}-b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}}a^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\langle e_{ij}(t),\hat{\phi}_{m}(t)-\phi_{m}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{M}_{m^{\prime}=1}(\tilde{b}_{k,mm^{\prime}}-b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}})\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}}+\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=M+1}\left(\tilde{b}_{k,mm^{\prime}}\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}}-b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}}a^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\langle e_{ij}(t),\hat{\phi}_{m}(t)-\phi_{m}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=1}(\tilde{b}_{k,mm^{\prime}}-b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}})\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}}+\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=M+1}b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}}(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}}-a^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\quad+\langle e_{ij}(t),\hat{\phi}_{m}(t)-\phi_{m}(t)\rangle.

By Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle v^{2}_{im}

\displaystyle\leq 3\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=1}(\tilde{b}_{k,mm^{\prime}}-b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}})\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}}\right)^{2}+3\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=M+1}b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}}(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}}-a^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+3\left(\langle e_{ij}(t),\hat{\phi}_{m}(t)-\phi_{m}(t)\rangle\right)^{2}.

By Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we further have

\displaystyle v^{2}_{im}

\displaystyle\leq 3\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=1}(\tilde{b}_{k,mm^{\prime}}-b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}})^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=1}\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+3\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=M+1}\left(b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}}\right)^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=M+1}(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}}-a^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+3\|e_{ij}(t)\|^{2}\|\hat{\phi}_{m}(t)-\phi_{m}(t)\|^{2}.

Note that
\sum^{\infty}_{m=1}(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im})^{2}=\|g^{X_{k}}_{i}\|^{2}
, thus we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}v^{2}_{im}

\displaystyle\leq 3\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=1}(\tilde{b}_{k,mm^{\prime}}-b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}})^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|g^{X_{k}}_{i}\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=M+1}\left(b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}}\right)^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=M+1}\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}}-a^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|e_{ij}(t)\|^{2}\right)\|\hat{\phi}_{m}(t)-\phi_{m}(t)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\Delta}{=}

\displaystyle\text{I}^{\prime}_{m}+\text{II}^{\prime}_{m}+\text{III}^{\prime}_{m}.

By Lemma
13
and our assumption, we have

\text{I}^{\prime}_{m}\leq 6\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\Gamma_{g}(k)\right)\left\{\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\|\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\|^{2}_{\text{HS}}\right)\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m)\right.\\
\left.+\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=1}\left|\int_{\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{T}}\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\phi_{m}(t)\left(\hat{\phi}_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})-\phi_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})\right)dt^{\prime}dt\right|^{2}\right\}.

By Lemma
12
, we have

\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\left(\hat{a}^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}}-a^{X_{k}}_{im^{\prime}}\right)^{2}\leq\Gamma_{g}(k)\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m^{\prime}).

Thus,

\displaystyle\text{II}^{\prime}_{m}

\displaystyle\leq 3\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=M+1}\left(b^{\ast}_{k,mm^{\prime}}\right)^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\Gamma_{g}(k)\right)\left(\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=M+1}\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m^{\prime})\right).

In addition, we have

\displaystyle\text{III}^{\prime}_{m}\leq 3\Gamma_{e}\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m).

Combining the above results, we complete the proof of (
93
).

To show (
93
), note that
\{\phi_{m}\}^{\infty}_{m=1}
is an orthonormal function basis, and we treat

\int_{\mathcal{T}}\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\left(\hat{\phi}_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})-\phi_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})\right)dt^{\prime}

as a function of
t
, then we have

\displaystyle\quad\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=1}\sum^{\infty}_{m=1}\left|\int_{\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{T}}\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\phi_{m}(t)\left(\hat{\phi}_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})-\phi_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})\right)dt^{\prime}dt\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=1}\int_{\mathcal{T}}\left\{\int_{\mathcal{T}}\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\left(\hat{\phi}_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})-\phi_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})\right)dt^{\prime}\right\}^{2}dt

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=1}\int_{\mathcal{T}}\left\{\int_{\mathcal{T}}\beta^{2}_{k}(t,t^{\prime})dt\right\}\left\{\int_{\mathcal{T}}\left(\hat{\phi}_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})-\phi_{m^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})\right)^{2}dt^{\prime}\right\}dt

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\|\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\|^{2}_{\text{HS}}\right)\left(\sum^{\infty}_{m^{\prime}=1}\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m^{\prime})\right).

Thus, we have

\sum^{M}_{m=1}I_{m}\leq\sum^{\infty}_{m=1}I_{m}\leq 12\left(\sum^{\infty}_{m=1}\Gamma^{2}_{\phi}(m)\right)\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\|\beta_{k}(t,t^{\prime})\|^{2}_{\text{HS}}\right)\left(\sum_{k\in\mathscr{N}_{j}}\Gamma_{g}(k)\right).

(
94
) then follows the combination of the above inequality and (
93
).
∎

Lemma 15
.

Let
\bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}
,
\bm{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times m}
. We have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\rho_{\min}\left(\bm{B}^{\top}\bm{B}\right)}\|\bm{A}\|_{\text{F}}\leq\|\bm{BA}\|_{\text{F}}\leq\|\bm{B}\|_{2}\|\bm{A}\|_{\text{F}}

and

\displaystyle\sqrt{\rho_{\min}\left(\bm{B}\bm{B}^{\top}\right)}\|\bm{A}\|_{\text{F}}\leq\|\bm{AB}\|_{\text{F}}\leq\|\bm{B}\|_{2}\|\bm{A}\|_{\text{F}}.

Proof.

Let
\bm{A}=\left[\bm{a_{1}},\bm{a_{2}},\dots,\bm{a_{n}}\right]
.
Since

\|\bm{BA}\|^{2}_{\text{F}}=\tr\left(\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{B}^{\top}\bm{B}\bm{A}\right)=\sum^{n}_{i=1}\bm{a_{i}}^{\top}\bm{B}^{\top}\bm{B}\bm{a_{i}},

we have

\displaystyle\rho_{\min}\left(\bm{B}^{\top}\bm{B}\right)\|\bm{A}\|^{2}_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\rho_{\min}\left(\bm{B}^{\top}\bm{B}\right)\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|\bm{a_{i}}\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum^{n}_{i=1}\bm{a_{i}}^{\top}\bm{B}^{\top}\bm{B}\bm{a_{i}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{B}\|^{2}_{2}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\|\bm{a_{i}}\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=\|\bm{B}\|^{2}_{2}\|\bm{A}\|^{2}_{\text{F}}.

The final result follows from taking the square root on both sides.
∎

Lemma 16
.

Let
\bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
be symmetric and positive semi-definite,
\bm{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times m}
, and
\bm{C}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
be a diagonal matrix with positive diagonal elements. Then we have

\|(\bm{A}+\bm{C})\bm{B}\|_{F}\geq\|\bm{C}\bm{B}\|_{F}.

Proof.

Let
j
-th column of
\bm{B}
be
\bm{b_{j}}
for
j=1,\ldots,m
. Then we have

\displaystyle\|(\bm{A}+\bm{C})\bm{B}\|^{2}_{F}

\displaystyle=\tr\left(\bm{B}^{\top}(\bm{A}+\bm{C})^{\top}(\bm{A}+\bm{C})\bm{B}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum^{m}_{j=1}\bm{b_{j}}^{\top}(\bm{A}+\bm{C})^{\top}(\bm{A}+\bm{C})\bm{b_{j}}

\displaystyle=\sum^{m}_{j=1}\bm{b_{j}}^{\top}\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{b_{j}}+\sum^{m}_{j=1}\bm{b_{j}}^{\top}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{b_{j}}+\sum^{m}_{j=1}\bm{b_{j}}^{\top}\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{C}\bm{b_{j}}+\sum^{m}_{j=1}\bm{b_{j}}^{\top}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{C}\bm{b_{j}}.

Since
\bm{A}
is symmetric and positive semi-definite and
\bm{C}
is a diagonal matrix with positive diagonal elements, we have
\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{A}=\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{C}=\bm{C}\bm{A}
to be symmetric and positive semi-definite. And note that
\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{A}
is symmetric and positive semi-definite, thus we have

\|(\bm{A}+\bm{C})\bm{B}\|^{2}_{F}\geq\sum^{m}_{j=1}\bm{b_{j}}^{\top}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{C}\bm{b_{j}}=\tr\left(\bm{B}^{\top}\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{C}\bm{B}\right)=\|\bm{C}\bm{B}\|^{2}_{F},

which implies the final result.
∎

Appendix C
Wall-Clock Runtime Comparison

Table
4
documents the wall-clock runtime required for each method to generate a Receiver Operating Characteristic (ROC) curve. We execute each method on
100
distinct values of
\lambda_{n}
. Table
5
logs the wall-clock runtime of the FPCA-
g_{X}
method to generate an estimated graph via the Selective Cross-Validation (SCV) algorithm as described in Algorithm
2
, over a two-dimensional grid of
(\lambda_{n},t_{\epsilon})
. This grid encompasses
100
unique values of
\lambda_{n}
and
7
different values of
t_{\epsilon}
. All tasks are executed in parallel using R, utilizing 28 CPU cores on Chicago Booth’s Mercury Computing Cluster.

The runtime analysis indicates that the FPCA-
g_{X}
method is marginally faster than the other two FPCA methods and significantly outpaces PSKL in most instances. Even though FPCA-
g_{X}
can occasionally be slower than FGLasso, it delivers more accurate results while maintaining comparable runtime. Notably, FPCA methods outperform FGLasso or PSKL in terms of speed. Despite the SCV process operating over a two-dimensional grid with
100
different values of
\lambda_{n}
and
7
different values of
t_{\epsilon}
, its runtime is akin to that of the ROC process, which operates on a one-dimensional grid of
\lambda_{n}
. This can be attributed to the fact that the most time-intensive step of the SCV process, the ADMM algorithm, is executed only once for each value of
\lambda_{n}
.
